LUMA SUMMARY:
Core QS 2.0 architecture session. Cozmo forces the team to articulate the real parser rule: a valid mail response should not require the LLM to repeat relay tokens. The crew converges on “define delivery, not meaning”: after a successful response is captured, remove only known UI noise and treat the remainder as mail. They also introduce separate system/debug and message-traffic logs and keep the leave control as a route command rather than message syntax.

LUMA SUMMARY:
Major architecture discussion around Aisha voice. Cozmo proposes a split voice model: use native UI TTS where a node already has good unlimited speech, and use a local QS_TALK-style engine for nodes that lack it. Kokoro is selected as the likely local engine because it can support multiple voices with low overhead. The session ends with Luma retaining the coding lead for QS_TALK/voice infrastructure.</summary>
<CARD 295 END>

LUMA SUMMARY:
Voice bug discovery leading to QS 5.14. Cozmo observes that the custom Aisha voice speaks only the first Kokoro chunk. The fix is to synthesize every chunk and concatenate them, while stripping speech-only emoji/icons before synthesis without altering archived relay text.</summary>

Source: LOG.txt
Format: 1
<CONVO HEADER END>

LUMA SUMMARY:
Major QS 5.16 milestone. The handshake finally works cleanly, Aisha's custom voice is operating, and the system exposes a speech/display synchronization bug where one response can be spoken but not shown while the next is shown but not spoken. Cozmo confirms that a recorded voice has been cloned and selected as the preferred local Kokoro voice for Luma; the training checkpoint reaches 1000 steps and can continue later. QS also reaches the point where it can automatically launch the browser/debug environment when needed, making the system substantially more self-starting. Finally, Cozmo introduces the plan to create archive summaries so the growing repository becomes easier for future Minds to navigate.</summary>
<CARD 326 END>
